- **Authors**: GigaChat team, Mamedov Valentin, Evgenii Kosarev, Gregory Leleytner, Ilya Shchuckin, Valeriy Berezovskiy, Daniil Smirnov, Dmitry Kozlov, Sergei Averkiev, Lukyanenko Ivan, Aleksandr Proshunin, Ainur Israfilova, Ivan Baskov, Artem Chervyakov, Emil Shakirov, Mikhail Kolesov, Daria Khomich, Darya Latortseva, Sergei Porkhun, Yury Fedorov, Oleg Kutuzov, Polina Kudriavtseva, Sofiia Soldatova, Kolodin Egor, Stanislav Pyatkin, Dzmitry Menshykh, Grafov Sergei, Eldar Damirov, Karlov Vladimir, Ruslan Gaitukiev, Arkadiy Shatenov, Alena Fenogenova, Nikita Savushkin, Fedor Minkin
- **Abstract**: Generative large language models (LLMs) have become crucial for modern NLP research and applications across various languages. However, the development of foundational models specifically tailored to the Russian language has been limited, primarily due to the significant computational resources required. This paper introduces the GigaChat family of Russian LLMs, available in various sizes, including base models and instruction-tuned versions. We provide a detailed report on the model architecture, pre-training process, and experiments to guide design choices. In addition, we evaluate their performance on Russian and English benchmarks and compare GigaChat with multilingual analogs. The paper presents a system demonstration of the top-performing models accessible via an API, a Telegram bot, and a Web interface. Furthermore, we have released three open GigaChat models in open-source (https://huggingface.co/ai-sage), aiming to expand NLP research opportunities and support the development of industrial solutions for the Russian language.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the GigaChat family of Russian language models (LLMs), focusing on models built using a Mixture of Experts (MoE) architecture. The authors detail their experience in creating the models, including the design choices, pre-training processes, experiments, and the development of a Russian-specific tokenizer. The paper evaluates the performance of GigaChat models on Russian and English benchmarks, comparing them to multilingual and Russian-specific counterparts. The authors also release three open-source versions of the GigaChat models, and provide access to proprietary, higher-performing models through API, Telegram bot, and a web interface. The paper emphasizes efficiency gains from using MoE, and provides insights into expert control and concentration mechanisms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation and open-sourcing of a family of MoE-based LLMs specifically designed for the Russian language. While MoE architectures are not entirely new, their application and release for Russian language modeling address a clear gap in the field, where foundational models have been limited due to resource constraints and lack of cultural adaptation. The description of a new tokenizer tailored for Cyrillic scripts, and the insights into MoE training (specifically expert control) contribute to the novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The work is significant because it:
    *   **Addresses a gap:** It tackles the under-representation of Russian language models and the cultural specifics of the language.
    *   **Releases open-source models:** This expands research opportunities in Russian NLP and supports the development of industrial solutions.
    *   **Provides a practical demonstration:** The availability of API, Telegram Bot, and Web interface allows for easy access and use of the models.
    *   **Contributes to understanding MoEs:** The paper provides experimental results and insights into MoE training, which can be valuable for researchers working with this architecture.
    * **Details Pre-training and Fine-tuning Processes:** The rigorous documentation of training steps, architecture, and evaluation is also a positive contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear presentation of model architecture and training processes.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation on a variety of benchmarks.
    *   Focus on a specific language (Russian) and cultural nuances.
    *   Open-sourcing of models and accessibility through various interfaces.
    * The introduction of the optimal tokenization that maximizes efficiency and balances performance across various domains.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper mentions the use of closed datasets for pre-training/fine-tuning in some models, hindering reproducibility. While open versions are released, some of the claims for state-of-the-art performance are based on models which are not fully transparent.
    *   Limited discussion of potential biases in the pre-training data beyond a general statement. A more thorough analysis of potential biases inherent in Russian internet content would strengthen the ethical considerations.
    * The paper only mentions the use of a censorship filter without describing it in detail, not specifying whether all models use it.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The GigaChat family has the potential to significantly influence the field of Russian NLP. Open-sourcing the models, tokenizer, and insights can encourage further research, development of new applications, and greater accessibility to language technology for Russian speakers. Specifically, the open version of the models, even though possibly not being on par with the proprietary version, can be used as a solid base for further work on Russian NLP research.
    The emphasis on efficiency through MoE can also drive research on resource-efficient language models.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by providing a family of Russian LLMs, particularly utilizing and open-sourcing the MoE architecture for this language. The release of the models, tokenizer, and training insights is significant and addresses a key limitation in the availability of resources for Russian NLP. However, the reliance on closed datasets for some models hinders complete reproducibility and the ethical considerations, although mentioned, can be strengthened. This lowers the score slightly as transparency is paramount in the current state of LLM research. Considering these aspects, a score of 7.5 reflects the paper's solid contribution and future potential impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Attention-Bayesian Hybrid Approach to Modular Multiple Particle Tracking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09441v1)
- **Authors**: Piyush Mishra, Philippe Roudot
- **Abstract**: Tracking multiple particles in noisy and cluttered scenes remains challenging due to a combinatorial explosion of trajectory hypotheses, which scales super-exponentially with the number of particles and frames. The transformer architecture has shown a significant improvement in robustness against this high combinatorial load. However, its performance still falls short of the conventional Bayesian filtering approaches in scenarios presenting a reduced set of trajectory hypothesis. This suggests that while transformers excel at narrowing down possible associations, they may not be able to reach the optimality of the Bayesian approach in locally sparse scenario. Hence, we introduce a hybrid tracking framework that combines the ability of self-attention to learn the underlying representation of particle behavior with the reliability and interpretability of Bayesian filtering. We perform trajectory-to-detection association by solving a label prediction problem, using a transformer encoder to infer soft associations between detections across frames. This prunes the hypothesis set, enabling efficient multiple-particle tracking in Bayesian filtering framework. Our approach demonstrates improved tracking accuracy and robustness against spurious detections, offering a solution for high clutter multiple particle tracking scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel hybrid framework, ABHA (Attention-Bayesian Hybrid Approach), for multiple particle tracking in challenging noisy and cluttered environments. ABHA combines the strengths of transformer-based self-attention mechanisms and Bayesian filtering.  Specifically, a transformer encoder is used to learn soft associations between detections across frames, effectively pruning the trajectory hypothesis set. This pruned hypothesis set is then fed into a Kalman filter for efficient multiple-particle tracking. The paper argues that transformers excel at narrowing down association possibilities, while Bayesian filtering is optimal for locally sparse scenarios.  Experiments on simulated data mimicking live-cell microscopy demonstrate that ABHA improves tracking accuracy and robustness compared to a purely Bayesian approach (MHT). The paper emphasizes that ABHA provides a good balance between accuracy and interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the hybrid architecture that strategically combines transformers and Bayesian filtering.  While both transformers and Bayesian filtering have been used individually in tracking, the *specific* combination and modular design presented here is a distinctive feature. Prior work from the authors on transformers for tracking provides a foundation but this expands on it significantly with the hybrid concept.  Also, the use of self-attention for *hypothesis pruning* prior to Bayesian filtering is a unique aspect. The architecture appears well-designed.

* **Significance:** Multiple particle tracking is a fundamental problem in various fields, especially live-cell imaging.  The proposed method addresses a key challenge: the combinatorial explosion of trajectory hypotheses in cluttered scenes. By improving tracking accuracy and robustness, ABHA could enable more reliable analysis of particle dynamics in complex biological environments. Furthermore, the modular design is a significant strength, as it is possible that other state estimation and tracking methods besides Kalman filtering can be utilized in future works.
The study uses point-congruence metrics (Link Precision, Link Recall), Jaccard Similarity coefficient, and TGOSPA metrics that would be standard to the particle tracking community. As such, this work would be easily interpreted by the community. However, there are a few limitations in its approach, such as the low-density scenarios, which are better optimized by more basic tools such as MHT. The authors also mention that ABHA is not positioned as a 'universal outperformer' which further limits the significance and impact.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Principled approach:** The motivation for the hybrid architecture is well-articulated, drawing on the relative strengths of transformers and Bayesian filtering.
    * **Clear methodology:** The paper provides a detailed description of the ABHA architecture and training procedure.
    * **Experimental validation:** The experimental results demonstrate improved performance compared to a baseline method (MHT) on relevant simulated datasets.
    * **Interpretability:** The paper emphasizes the interpretability of the framework, a key advantage over purely "black box" deep learning approaches.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses primarily on 2D tracking and a specific type of simulated particle motion. It's not clear how well the approach would generalize to 3D tracking or different types of particle dynamics. This said, the approach is easily generalizable for implementation on higher dimensional data.
    * **Evaluation:**  While the experimental results are promising, the evaluation could be strengthened by including a wider range of comparison methods and real-world datasets.  The simulation does not cover all scenarios and the comparison is primarily against a classic approach. A comparison with SOTA approaches, particularly deep-learning tracking methods, would substantially strengthen the evaluation.
    * **Computational cost:** The paper does not discuss the computational cost of ABHA compared to other methods.  Transformers can be computationally expensive, and it's important to assess the trade-off between accuracy and computational efficiency. Training takes ~21 hours which is long, and not easily generalizable.

* **Potential influence:** ABHA has the potential to influence the field of multiple particle tracking by demonstrating the effectiveness of combining deep learning and Bayesian filtering. The modular design could inspire further research on hybrid tracking frameworks.

**Score:** 7/10

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-motivated hybrid tracking framework with demonstrated improvements over a baseline method. The modular design and interpretability are significant strengths. However, the limited scope (2D tracking, specific motion types) and lack of comparison with other state-of-the-art methods somewhat limit the significance of the contribution. The potential for broader impact is there, but more extensive validation and analysis are needed to fully assess its value. Furthermore, the training time is excessively long which is another limiter. While not groundbreaking, it is certainly a noteworthy contribution, justifying the score.

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLMs Cannot Reliably Judge (Yet?): A Comprehensive Assessment on the Robustness of LLM-as-a-Judge
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09443v1)
- **Authors**: Songze Li, Chuokun Xu, Jiaying Wang, Xueluan Gong, Chen Chen, Jirui Zhang, Jun Wang, Kwok-Yan Lam, Shouling Ji
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable intelligence across various tasks, which has inspired the development and widespread adoption of LLM-as-a-Judge systems for automated model testing, such as red teaming and benchmarking. However, these systems are susceptible to adversarial attacks that can manipulate evaluation outcomes, raising concerns about their robustness and, consequently, their trustworthiness. Existing evaluation methods adopted by LLM-based judges are often piecemeal and lack a unified framework for comprehensive assessment. Furthermore, prompt template and model selections for improving judge robustness have been rarely explored, and their performance in real-world settings remains largely unverified. To address these gaps, we introduce RobustJudge, a fully automated and scalable framework designed to systematically evaluate the robustness of LLM-as-a-Judge systems. RobustJudge investigates the impact of attack methods and defense strategies (RQ1), explores the influence of prompt template and model selection (RQ2), and assesses the robustness of real-world LLM-as-a-Judge applications (RQ3).Our main findings are: (1) LLM-as-a-Judge systems are still vulnerable to a range of adversarial attacks, including Combined Attack and PAIR, while defense mechanisms such as Re-tokenization and LLM-based Detectors offer improved protection; (2) Robustness is highly sensitive to the choice of prompt template and judge models. Our proposed prompt template optimization method can improve robustness, and JudgeLM-13B demonstrates strong performance as a robust open-source judge; (3) Applying RobustJudge to Alibaba's PAI platform reveals previously unreported vulnerabilities. The source code of RobustJudge is provided at https://github.com/S3IC-Lab/RobustJudge.
- **Summary**: The paper "LLMs Cannot Reliably Judge (Yet?): A Comprehensive Assessment on the Robustness of LLM-as-a-Judge" introduces RobustJudge, a framework for evaluating the robustness of LLM-as-a-Judge systems against adversarial attacks. The paper systematically assesses these systems by exploring the impact of various attacks and defenses, the influence of prompt templates and model selection, and vulnerabilities in real-world deployments. The framework is used to evaluate several attack methods, and defense strategies, and to identify reliable LLM configurations against adversarial attacks. It also uncovers vulnerabilities in Alibaba's PAI platform.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper addresses an important and timely issue: the robustness of LLM-as-a-Judge systems. Given the increasing reliance on these systems, ensuring their reliability is crucial.
    *   RobustJudge provides a comprehensive and automated framework for evaluating LLM-as-a-Judge systems, which is a significant contribution in itself. The tool is made publicly available.
    *   The paper offers a systematic investigation of different attack methods and defense strategies, providing valuable insights into their relative strengths and weaknesses.
    *   The analysis of prompt template and model selection is thorough and reveals the sensitivity of LLM-as-a-Judge systems to these factors. The optimization method for prompt templates demonstrates potential for improving robustness.
    *   The real-world case study on Alibaba's PAI platform is valuable, as it uncovers previously unreported vulnerabilities.
    *   The research questions are well-defined, and the experiments are generally well-designed to address them.
    *   The paper is well-written and organized, making it easy to follow the methodology and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper is comprehensive, it mainly focuses on a specific set of attacks and defenses. There is a limited discussion on potential defenses that could be designed and applied directly to the models before they are used in judge scenarios.
    *   The optimization of the prompt template is limited to a simple coordinate ascent approach. More sophisticated optimization techniques, like Bayesian optimization or evolutionary algorithms, might lead to further improvements.
    *   The real-world case study, while valuable, is limited to a single platform. Evaluating other real-world deployments would further strengthen the conclusions.
    *   The metric iSDR has some issues. Specifically, a large and negative iSDR would occur if the SDR is low and the improvement in output quality of the generated code or natural language is high. A large improvement in output quality of generated code or natural language should result in a positive iSDR.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The paper makes a valuable contribution by systematically evaluating the robustness of LLM-as-a-Judge systems. This is crucial considering their rising adoption for model testing and automated benchmarks.
    *   The development of RobustJudge is a significant technical achievement, providing a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners in the field.
    *   The identification of prompt sensitivity and model selection impacts adds to the understanding of these systems.
    *   The real-world case study demonstrates the practical value of the framework in uncovering vulnerabilities.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper is likely to stimulate further research on the robustness of LLM-as-a-Judge systems and to encourage the development of more resilient evaluation methods.
    *   RobustJudge can be adopted by researchers and practitioners to assess and compare different LLM-as-a-Judge systems.
    *   The insights from the paper can inform the design and development of more secure and reliable LLM-based evaluation platforms.
    *   The disclosure of vulnerabilities in the PAI platform has the potential to improve the security of that system.

**Justification for Score:**
The paper presents a robust and comprehensive study on a highly relevant topic. The development of the RobustJudge framework, the systematic evaluation of attacks and defenses, the insight into prompt and model sensitivity, and the real-world case study, collectively contribute significantly to our understanding of LLM-as-a-Judge systems. While there are limitations, such as optimization technique used for prompt templates and defenses implemented directly to the models before they are used in judge scenarios, the work is substantial and is likely to influence future research and development in this area. It presents several findings with supporting data that improve the security, robustness, and deployment of judge-tuned models.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### UniToMBench: Integrating Perspective-Taking to Improve Theory of Mind in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09450v1)
- **Authors**: Prameshwar Thiyagarajan, Vaishnavi Parimi, Shamant Sai, Soumil Garg, Zhangir Meirbek, Nitin Yarlagadda, Kevin Zhu, Chris Kim
- **Abstract**: Theory of Mind (ToM), the ability to understand the mental states of oneself and others, remains a challenging area for large language models (LLMs), which often fail to predict human mental states accurately. In this paper, we introduce UniToMBench, a unified benchmark that integrates the strengths of SimToM and TOMBENCH to systematically improve and assess ToM capabilities in LLMs by integrating multi-interaction task designs and evolving story scenarios. Supported by a custom dataset of over 1,000 hand-written scenarios, UniToMBench combines perspective-taking techniques with diverse evaluation metrics to better stimulate social cognition in LLMs. Through evaluation, we observe that while models like GPT-4o and GPT-4o Mini show consistently high accuracy in tasks involving emotional and belief-related scenarios, with results usually above 80%, there is significant variability in their performance across knowledge-based tasks. These results highlight both the strengths and limitations of current LLMs in ToM-related tasks, underscoring the value of UniToMBench as a comprehensive tool for future development. Our code is publicly available here: https://github.com/Shamant/unifiedtombenchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UniToMBench, a unified benchmark for evaluating Theory of Mind (ToM) capabilities in large language models (LLMs). It combines the strengths of existing frameworks, SimToM (focusing on perspective-taking) and TOMBENCH (providing diverse evaluation metrics), along with a custom dataset of hand-written scenarios featuring multi-interaction tasks and evolving story narratives. The authors evaluate several LLMs, including GPT-4 models, Llama 3, and Gemma 2, both with and without the SimToM perspective-taking framework.  The results indicate that while LLMs perform well in structured tasks, they struggle in dynamic scenarios. SimToM can improve performance in certain areas, but also introduces new challenges related to handling ambiguity and implicit social understanding. The paper highlights the strengths and limitations of current LLMs in ToM-related tasks and positions UniToMBench as a comprehensive tool for future development in this area.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonable degree of novelty in its integrated approach to ToM benchmarking. Combining existing frameworks and adding a custom dataset is a valuable contribution. However, it's not fundamentally groundbreaking, as it builds upon existing methods. The types of scenarios they designed (evolving stories, multi-interaction tasks) are also not entirely novel concepts in the context of LLM evaluation, though the specific implementation and combination are original.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the creation of a more comprehensive and challenging benchmark for ToM in LLMs. The inclusion of multi-interaction and evolving story scenarios fills a gap in previous benchmarks that primarily focused on static, single-turn question-answering. This allows for a more realistic assessment of ToM capabilities in dynamic, real-world social interactions. The identification of specific failure patterns (misinterpretation of implicit cues, belief tracking in multi-agent scenarios, abstract reasoning limitations) is also valuable for guiding future research. The findings contribute to a better understanding of the strengths and weaknesses of LLMs in relation to human-like social reasoning.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The integration of existing frameworks and the addition of a new dataset create a more robust and comprehensive benchmark.
    *   The focus on multi-interaction and evolving story scenarios addresses a limitation of previous benchmarks.
    *   The error analysis provides valuable insights into the specific challenges that LLMs face in ToM tasks.
    *   The paper includes a good variety of models, which allows for a comparison across architectures.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dataset size, while decent, could be larger to further improve the reliability and generalizability of the results. The paper mentions a dataset of "over 1,000 annotated scenarios" which may not be significant considering the complexity of testing ToM.
    *   While providing a more dynamic testing setting, the paper can also investigate some additional tasks like planning, acting and goal-directed behaviors.
    *   The evaluation is largely based on accuracy. While accuracy is important, it would be more convincing if they added explanation generation and validation, providing justification and support to the answer.
    *   The paper primarily focuses on zero-shot evaluation. Fine-tuning experiments could provide further insights into the potential for improving ToM capabilities.
    *   The analysis could be more in-depth. For example, providing concrete examples of the model failures, and categorizing the failure types may strengthen the claims.

*   **Potential Influence:** The benchmark could potentially become a standard tool for evaluating ToM capabilities in LLMs, similar to how GLUE and SuperGLUE are used for general language understanding. The insights from the error analysis could inform the development of new training techniques and model architectures specifically designed to improve ToM. However, its influence will depend on its adoption by the research community and its ability to keep pace with advancements in LLMs.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a valuable but incremental contribution to the field of ToM in LLMs. The integrated benchmark addresses some limitations of existing methods and provides a more comprehensive evaluation framework. The design and use of novel tasks (multi-interaction, evolving stories) are also welcome additions. However, the approach is not fundamentally groundbreaking, and the study has some limitations in terms of dataset size, evaluation metrics, and the depth of analysis. Therefore, while useful and reasonably novel, a score of 6.5 reflects these limitations while acknowledging the contribution. The study’s value will increase if future work leverages this benchmark to explore new techniques for improving ToM, and if the benchmark gains wider adoption.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Learning Obfuscations Of LLM Embedding Sequences: Stained Glass Transform
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09452v1)
- **Authors**: Jay Roberts, Kyle Mylonakis, Sidhartha Roy, Kaan Kale
- **Abstract**: The high cost of ownership of AI compute infrastructure and challenges of robust serving of large language models (LLMs) has led to a surge in managed Model-as-a-service deployments. Even when enterprises choose on-premises deployments, the compute infrastructure is typically shared across many teams in order to maximize the return on investment. In both scenarios the deployed models operate only on plaintext data, and so enterprise data owners must allow their data to appear in plaintext on a shared or multi-tenant compute infrastructure. This results in data owners with private or sensitive data being hesitant or restricted in what data they use with these types of deployments. In this work we introduce the Stained Glass Transform, a learned, stochastic, and sequence dependent transformation of the word embeddings of an LLM which information theoretically provides privacy to the input of the LLM while preserving the utility of model. We theoretically connect a particular class of Stained Glass Transforms to the theory of mutual information of Gaussian Mixture Models. We then calculate a-postiori privacy estimates, based on mutual information, and verify the privacy and utility of instances of transformed embeddings through token level metrics of privacy and standard LLM performance benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces the Stained Glass Transform (SGT), a learned, stochastic, and sequence-dependent transformation applied to the word embeddings of a Large Language Model (LLM). The goal is to protect the privacy of the input data sent to the LLM for inference without significantly degrading the model's performance. The SGT adds noise to the embeddings based on learnable parameters, aiming to make it difficult to reconstruct the original input. The paper theoretically connects a specific class of SGTs to Gaussian Mixture Models and mutual information, deriving privacy estimates and experimentally verifying utility and privacy through token-level metrics and standard LLM benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The core idea of transforming embeddings instead of tokens directly is novel. It allows for continuous optimization techniques and potentially finer-grained privacy control compared to tokenization-based approaches.
    *   The theoretical link between a class of SGTs and Gaussian Mixture Models (GMMs) is a good theoretical contribution to the study of these models. It can allow for information-theoretic privacy analysis using well-understood tools.
    *   The paper's strength lies in its attempt to formalize and theoretically ground the privacy mechanism.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The problem addressed – protecting the privacy of data used for LLM inference – is highly significant. As LLMs become more ubiquitous, ensuring data privacy during inference is crucial, especially in multi-tenant or shared infrastructure environments.
    *   The practical implications of this research are potentially large. If the SGT can effectively protect data while maintaining LLM utility, it could enable broader adoption of LLMs in privacy-sensitive applications.
    *   The results, while promising, are not necessarily breakthrough. The paper offers a promising technique but there are still improvements needed to show significant benefit.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a relevant and important problem in LLM security and privacy.
    *   The SGT concept is innovative and opens up a new avenue for privacy-preserving LLM inference.
    *   Provides a theoretical analysis of privacy guarantees, linking SGTs to GMMs and mutual information.
    *   Experiments demonstrate a good balance between privacy and utility.
    *   Includes comprehensive experimentation and ablation studies demonstrating the performance impact of the components of SGTs.
    *   Offers a detailed view of different adversarial attacks that could be possible and shows how to protect again these attacks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper's reliance on mutual information as the core privacy metric could be a weakness. As the authors themselves note, mutual information bounds can be loose, and stronger practical privacy guarantees might be necessary for real-world deployment. It is clear that there are still areas for improvement, especially regarding providing mathematically sound privacy guarantees for SGTs.
    *   The practical privacy evaluations (nearest neighbor reconstruction) are somewhat basic and may not capture the full range of potential attacks. More sophisticated adversarial attacks would strengthen the paper.
    *   The complexity of the mutual information loss function makes optimization challenging, requiring careful tuning of hyperparameters.
    *   The evaluation is primarily on benchmarks, where performance may not translate into real-world application, such as using the LLM for tasks such as information extraction or analysis.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The SGT approach could inspire further research into embedding-based privacy mechanisms for LLMs.
    *   The theoretical analysis of SGTs and GMMs could be valuable for other privacy-preserving techniques.
    *   The paper's findings on the trade-offs between privacy and utility could inform the design of future privacy-preserving LLM systems.

*   **Score Justification:**

    This paper makes a solid, if incremental, contribution to the field of privacy-preserving LLMs. The novelty is present in the SGT concept and the theoretical analysis, but the practical evaluations and privacy guarantees could be strengthened. The paper addresses an important problem and has the potential to influence future research. However, the limitations regarding mutual information bounds and the optimization complexity prevent it from being a truly exceptional contribution.

### Towards Bridging the Reward-Generation Gap in Direct Alignment Algorithms
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09457v1)
- **Authors**: Zeguan Xiao, Yun Chen, Guanhua Chen
- **Abstract**: Direct Alignment Algorithms (DAAs), such as Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) and Simple Preference Optimization (SimPO), have emerged as efficient alternatives to Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) algorithms for aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences. However, DAAs suffer from a fundamental limitation we identify as the "reward-generation gap" -- a misalignment between optimization objectives during training and actual generation performance during inference. In this paper, we find a contributor to the reward-generation gap is the mismatch between the inherent importance of prefix tokens during the LLM generation process and how this importance is reflected in the implicit reward functions of DAAs. To bridge the gap, we introduce a simple yet effective approach called Prefix-Oriented Equal-length Training (POET), which truncates both preferred and dispreferred responses to match the shorter one's length. Training with POET, where both responses in each sample are truncated to equal length, resulting in diverse truncated lengths across samples, the optimization of DAAs objective is implicitly constrained to converge across all positions, thus paying more attention to prefix tokens than the standard DAAs. We conduct experiments with DPO and SimPO, two representative DAAs, demonstrating that POET improves over their standard implementations, achieving up to 15.6 points in AlpacaEval 2 and overall improvements across downstream tasks. Our results highlight the importance of addressing the misalignment between reward optimization and generation performance in DAAs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses a fundamental limitation in Direct Alignment Algorithms (DAAs) for aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences, which the authors term the "reward-generation gap." This gap stems from a misalignment between the optimization objectives during training and the actual generation performance during inference. The authors argue that a key contributor to this gap is the mismatch between the importance of prefix tokens in LLM generation and how this importance is reflected in DAA's implicit reward functions. They propose Prefix-Oriented Equal-length Training (POET), a simple data augmentation technique that truncates both preferred and dispreferred responses to match the shorter one's length. By doing so, POET implicitly constrains the DAA's objective to converge across all positions, thereby paying more attention to prefix tokens.  Experiments with DPO and SimPO demonstrate improvements over standard implementations, achieving better performance on AlpacaEval 2 and downstream tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel concept, the "reward-generation gap," and offers a plausible explanation for its existence, focusing on the differential importance of prefix tokens. The POET technique is also novel, although relatively simple. Truncating responses is not a new data augmentation strategy in general, but the *prefix-oriented, equal-length* aspect is specific to this problem and nicely motivated. Prior work has focused on issues like likelihood over-optimization and reward model vulnerabilities; this paper presents a different angle.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the reward-generation gap is a significant problem as it directly impacts the effectiveness of DAAs, which are increasingly popular alternatives to RLHF. While the proposed POET method is relatively simple, its demonstrated improvements across various models and datasets suggest that the underlying issue is important and that even a simple intervention can yield benefits.  The paper also highlights the limitations of current reward functions in capturing the nuances of LLM generation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:** The reward-generation gap is well-defined and motivated.
    *   **Simple and effective solution:** POET is easy to implement and doesn't introduce new hyperparameters.
    *   **Strong empirical results:**  The improvements shown on AlpacaEval 2 and downstream tasks are compelling. The ablation analysis helps to understand why POET works and when it's most effective.
    *   **Good analysis:** The prefix quality analysis and the connection to quality differences in the preference dataset provides a solid understanding of the method's behavior.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simplicity of the solution:** While a strength, the simplicity of POET might also be seen as a weakness.  More sophisticated methods for dynamically weighting tokens based on their importance may yield greater gains, though they would also be more complex. The authors themselves acknowledge this in the limitations section.
    *   **Limited Scope:**  The reward-generation gap is explored from a single angle (prefix tokens). Other factors likely contribute to the problem, which are not addressed.
    *   **Reliance on strong reward models:** The analysis relies on having a strong reward model to estimate prefix quality. This is reasonable, but worth noting.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on DAAs.  It highlights the importance of aligning training objectives with generation performance and suggests that focusing on prefix tokens is a fruitful direction.  It also opens up the possibility of exploring more sophisticated methods for addressing the reward-generation gap.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a clearly defined problem with a well-motivated and empirically validated solution. The limitations of the approach are acknowledged, and the potential for future research is discussed. While the solution itself is simple, it effectively addresses a complex issue in a practically relevant area of LLM alignment. This justifies a higher-than-average score, but not the highest, given the simplicity of the proposed solution. The analysis is well-structured and provides insights into the workings of the proposed methods.

### ArcNeural: A Multi-Modal Database for the Gen-AI Era
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09467v1)
- **Authors**: Wu Min, Qiao Yuncong, Yu Tan, Chenghu Yang
- **Abstract**: ArcNeural introduces a novel multimodal database tailored for the demands of Generative AI and Large Language Models, enabling efficient management of diverse data types such as graphs, vectors, and documents. Its storage-compute separated architecture integrates graph technology, advanced vector indexing, and transaction processing to support real-time analytics and AI-driven applications. Key features include a unified storage layer, adaptive edge collection in MemEngine, and seamless integration of transaction and analytical processing. Experimental evaluations demonstrate ArcNeural's superior performance and scalability compared to state-of-the-art systems. This system bridges structured and unstructured data management, offering a versatile solution for enterprise-grade AI applications. ArcNeural's design addresses the challenges of multimodal data processing, providing a robust framework for intelligent, data-driven solutions in the Gen AI era.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ArcNeural, a multi-modal database system designed for the Gen-AI era. ArcNeural aims to address the challenges of integrating and managing diverse data types (graphs, vectors, documents) in AI applications. Key features include a storage-compute separated architecture, a unified storage layer ("log as a database"), adaptive edge collection in MemEngine for efficient graph topology handling, and a unified query interface for both transaction processing (TP) and analytical processing (AP).  The system integrates vector indexing leveraging HNSW.  The paper details the system architecture, data model, query language extensions, and discusses several architectural innovations including the use of "log as a database," MemEngine for accelerated topology processing, MPP for scalable query execution, and a unified HTAP approach. Preliminary experimental evaluations are alluded to but details are lacking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of multi-modal data support (graphs, vectors, documents) within a database system is not entirely novel, as existing systems like JanusGraph and various vector databases offer some of these capabilities.  The approach of using "log as a database" and the compute/storage separation is a common trend, although its specific implementation details in ArcNeural could offer incremental innovation. The adaptive edge collection in MemEngine seems a more interesting contribution.  Integrating HNSW indexes is standard practice, so its inclusion isn't inherently novel unless specific optimizations are made in its integration.

*   **Significance:**  The paper argues that ArcNeural is important for the Gen-AI era, facilitating intelligent, data-driven applications.  A major issue is the lack of detailed experimental results.  Without robust empirical evidence demonstrating superior performance or scalability compared to existing systems, the significance of ArcNeural is difficult to ascertain. Claims of superior performance are made without providing supporting data.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Architecture:** The paper presents a clear high-level architecture of the system.
    *   **Adaptive Edge Collection:** The MemEngine adaptive edge collection appears to be a reasonable approach to optimize graph storage based on vertex degree distribution.
    *   **Unified Query Interface:** Attempting to unify TP and AP through a common query interface is a worthwhile goal.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Lack of Detail:** While the architecture is described, many crucial implementation details are missing.  For example, the specific vectorization techniques, the implementation of the unified query interface, and the data flow between TP and AP are not sufficiently detailed.
    *   **Weak Evaluation:** The "Experimental Evaluation" section is listed as a TODO. Tables exist, but they lack context and clear interpretation. Without a well-designed experimental evaluation that demonstrates performance against existing solutions, it is difficult to judge the practical impact of ArcNeural. The performance comparisons of using Box<Vec> and Box<BTreeSet> do not include the baseline of just a Vec and just a BTreeSet.
    *   **Overstated Claims:** Claims of superior performance and scalability are made without sufficient justification.
    *   **Missing Related Work:** The related work section is too general and doesn't sufficiently position ArcNeural with respect to other multi-modal database systems and graph databases with vector support.
    *   **Unclear Implementation:** The language is sometimes vague making it hard to assess what is actually implemented and what is future work. For example, sections such as "Recovery and Fault Tolerance" seem to only restate what is normally done and not provide specifics of the system.

*   **Potential Impact:** If the system delivers on its promises of performance and scalability, it could contribute to more efficient and powerful AI applications. However, this is highly dependent on the actual implementation and results.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the lack of experimental results, the missing implementation details, and the incremental nature of the contributions, the paper's impact and novelty are questionable. While the architecture appears sound, the paper lacks the empirical evidence needed to support its claims and differentiate itself from existing solutions.  The adaptive edge collection strategy shows some promise, but again lacks detailed evaluation.

### Provoking Multi-modal Few-Shot LVLM via Exploration-Exploitation In-Context Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09473v1)
- **Authors**: Cheng Chen, Yunpeng Zhai, Yifan Zhao, Jinyang Gao, Bolin Ding, Jia Li
- **Abstract**: In-context learning (ICL), a predominant trend in instruction learning, aims at enhancing the performance of large language models by providing clear task guidance and examples, improving their capability in task understanding and execution. This paper investigates ICL on Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) and explores the policies of multi-modal demonstration selection. Existing research efforts in ICL face significant challenges: First, they rely on pre-defined demonstrations or heuristic selecting strategies based on human intuition, which are usually inadequate for covering diverse task requirements, leading to sub-optimal solutions; Second, individually selecting each demonstration fails in modeling the interactions between them, resulting in information redundancy. Unlike these prevailing efforts, we propose a new exploration-exploitation reinforcement learning framework, which explores policies to fuse multi-modal information and adaptively select adequate demonstrations as an integrated whole. The framework allows LVLMs to optimize themselves by continually refining their demonstrations through self-exploration, enabling the ability to autonomously identify and generate the most effective selection policies for in-context learning. Experimental results verify the superior performance of our approach on four Visual Question-Answering (VQA) datasets, demonstrating its effectiveness in enhancing the generalization capability of few-shot LVLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of demonstration selection for in-context learning (ICL) with Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). It proposes an exploration-exploitation reinforcement learning framework to adaptively select relevant demonstrations for a given query.  Unlike existing methods that rely on pre-defined demonstrations or heuristic selection strategies based on similarity, this framework learns a sampling policy network that explores multiple demonstration combinations using a stochastic beam search and then exploits promising combinations based on rewards obtained from the LVLM. The reward is based on the correctness of the LVLM's output given the selected demonstrations. The framework uses multi-modal interactive encoding to capture relationships between queries and candidate demonstrations.  Experiments on VQA datasets demonstrate that the proposed approach outperforms existing methods, including similarity-based retrieval, by enhancing the generalization capability of few-shot LVLMs.  The paper argues that considering the inter-sample relationships among demonstrations mitigates information redundancy and improves demo utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in framing the demonstration selection problem as a combinatorial optimization problem and tackling it with an exploration-exploitation reinforcement learning approach. This is a departure from the more common similarity-based or manually curated demonstration selection strategies. The idea of using stochastic beam search to explore different demonstration combinations and then using the LVLM itself to evaluate and reinforce those combinations is also novel.  The multi-modal interactive encoding is also a significant contribution, allowing for richer representations of image and text information.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial aspect of ICL in LVLMs, which is selecting appropriate demonstrations.  Improving demonstration selection can significantly enhance the performance and generalization ability of LVLMs in few-shot settings, reducing the need for extensive fine-tuning. The experimental results validate the effectiveness of the proposed framework on multiple VQA datasets, demonstrating practical value. The focus on reducing redundancy and enhancing demo utility addresses a key limitation in current ICL research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper offers a well-defined problem formulation.
    *   The proposed exploration-exploitation framework is novel and addresses the limitations of existing methods.
    *   The multi-modal interactive encoding effectively captures cross-modal relationships.
    *   The use of reinforcement learning for adaptive demonstration selection is innovative.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive and demonstrate the superior performance of the proposed approach.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different components of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reinforcement learning component adds complexity.  While the paper aims for a training-free alternative to fine-tuning, the policy network itself requires training. The reliance on specific hyperparameters related to the stochastic beam search and reward normalization could limit the generalizability or ease of use in other domains.
    *   The paper mentions the increased number of tokens introduced, which raises concerns about efficiency, but doesn't address how to deal with the increased inference costs. While the improvement in VQA performance is highlighted, the computational trade-offs are not thoroughly explored.
    *   While the qualitative examples provide some intuition, a more in-depth analysis of the types of demonstrations the model learns to select, beyond just similarity and diversity, would further strengthen the findings. More analysis on why this works would be good.
    * The limitation that the method explores language-vision modalities only are valid but they should also consider how can the proposed model tackle the problem of spurious correlation.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research on ICL in LVLMs by shifting the focus from heuristic selection strategies to learnable policies. The exploration-exploitation framework provides a general approach that can be adapted to different tasks and datasets.  The emphasis on considering the interactions between demonstrations and reducing redundancy is a valuable contribution to the field.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to demonstration selection in LVLMs using a reinforcement learning framework. The exploration-exploitation strategy, coupled with multi-modal encoding, addresses key limitations of existing methods and achieves significant performance improvements on VQA tasks. The ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed components.

However, the complexity of the reinforcement learning component, including the hyperparameter tuning and training requirements, somewhat reduces the practical appeal of the method. A more detailed analysis of the learned selection policies and a discussion of the computational trade-offs would further strengthen the paper. While promising, the paper stops short of being a truly groundbreaking contribution, hence the score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Marrying Autoregressive Transformer and Diffusion with Multi-Reference Autoregression
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09482v1)
- **Authors**: Dingcheng Zhen, Qian Qiao, Tan Yu, Kangxi Wu, Ziwei Zhang, Siyuan Liu, Shunshun Yin, Ming Tao
- **Abstract**: We introduce TransDiff, the first image generation model that marries Autoregressive (AR) Transformer with diffusion models. In this joint modeling framework, TransDiff encodes labels and images into high-level semantic features and employs a diffusion model to estimate the distribution of image samples. On the ImageNet 256x256 benchmark, TransDiff significantly outperforms other image generation models based on standalone AR Transformer or diffusion models. Specifically, TransDiff achieves a Fr\'echet Inception Distance (FID) of 1.61 and an Inception Score (IS) of 293.4, and further provides x2 faster inference latency compared to state-of-the-art methods based on AR Transformer and x112 faster inference compared to diffusion-only models. Furthermore, building on the TransDiff model, we introduce a novel image generation paradigm called Multi-Reference Autoregression (MRAR), which performs autoregressive generation by predicting the next image. MRAR enables the model to reference multiple previously generated images, thereby facilitating the learning of more diverse representations and improving the quality of generated images in subsequent iterations. By applying MRAR, the performance of TransDiff is improved, with the FID reduced from 1.61 to 1.42. We expect TransDiff to open up a new frontier in the field of image generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TransDiff, a novel image generation model that combines Autoregressive (AR) Transformers with diffusion models. TransDiff encodes labels and images into high-level semantic features using an AR Transformer and then employs a diffusion model to decode these features into images. The authors also propose Multi-Reference Autoregression (MRAR), a new autoregressive generation paradigm where the model predicts a complete image at each step, referencing multiple previously generated images for richer context.  Experiments on ImageNet demonstrate that TransDiff with MRAR achieves state-of-the-art results in terms of FID and Inception Score, along with faster inference speeds compared to diffusion-only models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of combining an AR transformer for feature extraction/encoding with a diffusion model for decoding is reasonably novel.  While both AR Transformers and diffusion models are individually well-established, their joint use in the proposed way is a valuable direction.The MRAR component, though conceptually straightforward, does add a new strategy within the autoregressive image generation space.

*   **Significance:** The results, especially the reported FID scores and faster inference, are significant. The claim of outperforming existing AR and diffusion-based models on ImageNet is noteworthy, if substantiated. A fast and high-quality image generation model is a valuable contribution with various potential applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical results: The paper presents quantitative results (FID, IS) that are competitive or superior to existing methods.
    *   Efficient inference: The claim of significantly faster inference compared to diffusion-only models is a substantial advantage for practical applications.
    *   MRAR improves the quality by referencing multiple images instead of a single blurry one.
    *   The method seems well-engineered, combining different components effectively.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Incremental improvements: Although the results are good, the overall contribution might be considered incremental rather than revolutionary. The paper combines existing techniques (AR Transformers, diffusion models) with a new autoregressive method.
    *   Limited analysis: Some claims are not sufficiently validated. For example, the "high-level semantic features" are not thoroughly analyzed to understand *what* semantics are being captured and how they contribute to the improved performance.
    *   Dataset limitations: The paper is heavily focused on ImageNet. Wider generalization experiments on different datasets.
    *   The training details can be improved. The number of epochs after switching to the MRAR paradigm is relatively low.

*   **Impact and Influence:**
    *   The paper could influence future research by demonstrating the potential of combining AR Transformers and diffusion models.
    *   MRAR introduces a new strategy for autoregressive image generation that can be adopted by other researchers.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. This score reflects the paper's contribution of a novel architecture achieving significant performance gains. While the core components are known, their effective integration and the introduction of MRAR represent a non-trivial engineering and algorithmic advance. The reported results on ImageNet, including both performance and efficiency benefits, validate the method's potential. However, the limitations prevent a higher score. More rigorous analysis of the learned features, broader dataset evaluation, and a deeper theoretical understanding of why MRAR is effective would increase the impact. The incremental nature of the improvements also moderates the score.

### Fast Monte Carlo Tree Diffusion: 100x Speedup via Parallel Sparse Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09498v1)
- **Authors**: Jaesik Yoon, Hyeonseo Cho, Yoshua Bengio, Sungjin Ahn
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have recently emerged as a powerful approach for trajectory planning. However, their inherently non-sequential nature limits their effectiveness in long-horizon reasoning tasks at test time. The recently proposed Monte Carlo Tree Diffusion (MCTD) offers a promising solution by combining diffusion with tree-based search, achieving state-of-the-art performance on complex planning problems. Despite its strengths, our analysis shows that MCTD incurs substantial computational overhead due to the sequential nature of tree search and the cost of iterative denoising. To address this, we propose Fast-MCTD, a more efficient variant that preserves the strengths of MCTD while significantly improving its speed and scalability. Fast-MCTD integrates two techniques: Parallel MCTD, which enables parallel rollouts via delayed tree updates and redundancy-aware selection; and Sparse MCTD, which reduces rollout length through trajectory coarsening. Experiments show that Fast-MCTD achieves up to 100x speedup over standard MCTD while maintaining or improving planning performance. Remarkably, it even outperforms Diffuser in inference speed on some tasks, despite Diffuser requiring no search and yielding weaker solutions. These results position Fast-MCTD as a practical and scalable solution for diffusion-based inference-time reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Fast Monte Carlo Tree Diffusion" (Fast-MCTD), an improved algorithm for trajectory planning that builds upon the existing Monte Carlo Tree Diffusion (MCTD) method.  MCTD combines diffusion models with tree-based search to achieve strong performance in long-horizon reasoning tasks. However, MCTD is computationally expensive.  Fast-MCTD addresses this inefficiency by incorporating two key techniques: Parallel MCTD (P-MCTD) and Sparse MCTD (S-MCTD).  P-MCTD parallelizes rollouts and defers tree updates, reducing between-rollout inefficiency. S-MCTD shortens rollout length by planning over coarsened trajectories, addressing within-rollout inefficiency.  Experimental results demonstrate significant speedups (up to 100x) compared to standard MCTD, with comparable or improved planning performance. In some tasks, Fast-MCTD even outperforms Diffuser, showcasing the benefits of incorporating search.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Real Bottleneck:**  The paper directly tackles a critical limitation of MCTD: its computational cost.  MCTD, while powerful, is impractical for many real-world scenarios due to this overhead. Identifying and addressing the "Planning Horizon Dilemma" is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Novel Combination of Techniques:**  The parallelization and sparsification strategies are well-motivated and effectively integrated.  The delayed tree update with redundancy-aware selection in P-MCTD is a clever way to balance parallelism and search quality. Sparse MCTD by coarsening trajectory also presents another efficient way to improve planning.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The speedups achieved by Fast-MCTD are impressive, clearly demonstrating its practical benefits. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the effectiveness of the individual components (P-MCTD and S-MCTD) and the importance of hyperparameter tuning. Showing the improved efficiency against Diffuser is also meaningful since Diffuser does not require search.
    *   **Well-written and Clear:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the algorithms and experimental setup.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is effective, the individual components (parallelization and trajectory coarsening) are not entirely novel in the broader context of MCTS or planning. Prior works have explored similar ideas in other domains. The novelty lies primarily in adapting and optimizing them for the diffusion-based planning setting. The authors need to carefully show their novelty in each component considering that the previous methods already use similar techniques.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the paper claims robustness across a range of hyperparameter values, the ablation studies do show some sensitivity to parameters like parallelism degree and interval size. Further analysis of these sensitivities and guidelines for choosing appropriate values would be beneficial. The robustness claim needs to be more rigorously substantiated.
    *   **Limited Task Diversity:** The experiments focus heavily on maze navigation and robotic manipulation. While these are relevant benchmarks, it would be helpful to see Fast-MCTD evaluated on a wider range of tasks, particularly those that highlight the long-horizon reasoning capabilities of diffusion models.

*   **Significance:**

    *   Fast-MCTD significantly improves the practicality of diffusion-based planning. By reducing computational cost, it makes MCTD more accessible for real-world applications.
    *   The paper highlights the importance of considering computational efficiency when designing inference-time scaling methods for diffusion models.
    *   The work contributes to the growing body of research on combining diffusion models with search algorithms, opening up new avenues for developing intelligent agents.

*   **Justification:**

    Fast-MCTD is a solid engineering contribution that addresses a significant bottleneck in an important area of research. The work lacks groundbreaking theoretical insights, however, its practical impact is significant. The techniques are well-motivated, and the empirical results are convincing. The analysis of the Planning Horizon Dilemma is insightful. While the components may not be entirely new on their own, their integration and adaptation to the diffusion-based planning domain represent a non-trivial contribution. It is more incremental than a paradigm shift, but makes MCTD more applicable.
