### Learning Efficient and Generalizable Graph Retriever for Knowledge-Graph Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09645v1)
- **Authors**: Tianjun Yao, Haoxuan Li, Zhiqiang Shen, Pan Li, Tongliang Liu, Kun Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown strong inductive reasoning ability across various domains, but their reliability is hindered by the outdated knowledge and hallucinations. Retrieval-Augmented Generation mitigates these issues by grounding LLMs with external knowledge; however, most existing RAG pipelines rely on unstructured text, limiting interpretability and structured reasoning. Knowledge graphs, which represent facts as relational triples, offer a more structured and compact alternative. Recent studies have explored integrating knowledge graphs with LLMs for knowledge graph question answering (KGQA), with a significant proportion adopting the retrieve-then-reasoning paradigm. In this framework, graph-based retrievers have demonstrated strong empirical performance, yet they still face challenges in generalization ability. In this work, we propose RAPL, a novel framework for efficient and effective graph retrieval in KGQA. RAPL addresses these limitations through three aspects: (1) a two-stage labeling strategy that combines heuristic signals with parametric models to provide causally grounded supervision; (2) a model-agnostic graph transformation approach to capture both intra- and inter-triple interactions, thereby enhancing representational capacity; and (3) a path-based reasoning strategy that facilitates learning from the injected rational knowledge, and supports downstream reasoner through structured inputs. Empirically, RAPL outperforms state-of-the-art methods by $2.66\%-20.34\%$, and significantly reduces the performance gap between smaller and more powerful LLM-based reasoners, as well as the gap under cross-dataset settings, highlighting its superior retrieval capability and generalizability. Codes are available at: https://github.com/tianyao-aka/RAPL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Learning Efficient and Generalizable Graph Retriever for Knowledge-Graph Question Answering":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RAPL, a novel framework designed to enhance the generalization ability of graph retrievers used in Knowledge Graph Question Answering (KGQA). RAPL tackles limitations of existing approaches by focusing on three key aspects: (1) a two-stage labeling strategy combining heuristic signals with parametric models (LLMs) for causally grounded supervision, (2) a model-agnostic graph transformation approach (line graph transformation) to capture both intra- and inter-triple interactions to enhance representational capacity, and (3) a path-based reasoning strategy that facilitates learning from rational knowledge and supports downstream reasoners with structured input.  Empirical results demonstrate that RAPL outperforms state-of-the-art methods, reduces the performance gap between different sized LLMs, and improves cross-dataset generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper presents a well-integrated approach, combining known techniques (GNNs, line graphs, LLMs) in a novel way to address a specific problem in KGQA. The *integration* is where the novelty lies.  The use of LLMs for rational path labeling is a relatively new approach to creating better supervision signals. The line graph transformation with emphasis on preserving directionality and enabling path-based reasoning is also a significant contribution. While line graph transformations are not new, their targeted application and articulation within a KGQA framework, especially for enhancing expressiveness and path-based reasoning is a novel application.

*   **Significance:** The problem of improving the generalization of graph retrievers in KGQA is significant. Many existing systems are highly dataset-specific and/or rely on potentially noisy heuristics. RAPL's ability to narrow the gap between smaller and more powerful LLMs is also important, as it makes high-performing KGQA systems more accessible and resource-efficient. The method's impact on cross-dataset generalizability offers tangible improvements. These advancements collectively move towards more robust, reliable, and generalizable KGQA systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper demonstrates substantial performance gains compared to state-of-the-art methods on widely used KGQA benchmarks.
    *   **Well-Justified Design Choices:**  The motivation for each component of RAPL (rational labeling, line graph transformation, path-based reasoning) is clearly explained, and the connections between them are logically presented.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  Ablation studies effectively analyze the contribution of each component, providing insights into their individual impact on overall performance.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper includes an in-depth analysis of RAPL's performance on questions with varying reasoning hops, faithfulness evaluations, and cross-dataset generalization. This demonstrates a commitment to rigorous evaluation beyond headline numbers.
    *   **Efficiency:** The paper addresses efficiency concerns. The retrievers are lightweight and the architecture avoids the high costs of many LLM-intensive agents.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** While the paper mitigates some issues with LLMs, the rational labeling process *still* relies on their performance. The quality of supervision is ultimately tied to the LLM annotator's capabilities. Future work could explore more robust and potentially self-supervised methods for identifying rational paths.
    *   **Limited Graph Retriever Architectures:** The paper primarily uses GCN as the underlying graph retriever. Examining different GNN architectures (Graph Attention Networks, etc.) might reveal even greater performance gains. The model-agnostic label may perform differently.
    *   **Assumption of Correct Question Entity Linking:** The paper assumes the question entity is correctly linked to the KG. In a real-world application, entity linking is a non-trivial problem. This implicit assumption may limit the general applicability in realistic KGQA systems.

*   **Potential Influence:**  RAPL's emphasis on rationalized training data, expressiveness of the retrieved graph, and path-based reasoning are likely to influence future research in KGQA and related areas like retrieval-augmented generation. The framework offers a solid foundation for building more robust and generalizable KGQA systems.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of KGQA by addressing the important issue of generalization in graph retrievers. The novelty lies in the clever integration of LLMs for rational supervision, the model-agnostic line graph transformation, and path-based reasoning strategy. The thorough empirical evaluation provides strong evidence for the effectiveness of RAPL. However, the reliance on LLMs for labeling, the use of limited graph retrieval architectures, and the assumption of correct question entity linking hold it back from receiving a higher score. Nonetheless, the paper represents a valuable advance and is likely to have a positive impact on future research in KGQA.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DipLLM: Fine-Tuning LLM for Strategic Decision-making in Diplomacy
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09655v1)
- **Authors**: Kaixuan Xu, Jiajun Chai, Sicheng Li, Yuqian Fu, Yuanheng Zhu, Dongbin Zhao
- **Abstract**: Diplomacy is a complex multiplayer game that requires both cooperation and competition, posing significant challenges for AI systems. Traditional methods rely on equilibrium search to generate extensive game data for training, which demands substantial computational resources. Large Language Models (LLMs) offer a promising alternative, leveraging pre-trained knowledge to achieve strong performance with relatively small-scale fine-tuning. However, applying LLMs to Diplomacy remains challenging due to the exponential growth of possible action combinations and the intricate strategic interactions among players. To address this challenge, we propose DipLLM, a fine-tuned LLM-based agent that learns equilibrium policies for Diplomacy. DipLLM employs an autoregressive factorization framework to simplify the complex task of multi-unit action assignment into a sequence of unit-level decisions. By defining an equilibrium policy within this framework as the learning objective, we fine-tune the model using only 1.5% of the data required by the state-of-the-art Cicero model, surpassing its performance. Our results demonstrate the potential of fine-tuned LLMs for tackling complex strategic decision-making in multiplayer games.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DipLLM, a fine-tuned Large Language Model (LLM) specifically designed for strategic decision-making in the complex multiplayer game Diplomacy.  DipLLM overcomes challenges related to Diplomacy's massive action space by employing an autoregressive factorization framework, breaking down the decision process into a sequence of unit-level actions. The model is fine-tuned using a Diplomacy-specific dataset and a defined equilibrium policy as the learning objective.  Experimental results demonstrate that DipLLM outperforms existing domain-specific models, including the state-of-the-art Cicero agent, while requiring significantly less training data (only 1.5% of Cicero's data). The paper also includes theoretical analysis supporting the proposed learning objective and provides ablation studies to evaluate the contribution of various components.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the application of fine-tuned LLMs combined with an autoregressive factorization approach to tackle a particularly challenging strategic multiplayer game. While LLMs have been used in games before, the specific combination of fine-tuning, autoregressive decomposition tailored to the Diplomacy's complexities, and theoretical grounding is relatively novel. The paper presents a distinct approach compared to pure prompting techniques or imitation learning-based methods.
*   **Significance:**  The results are significant because they demonstrate the potential of LLMs, when properly adapted, to achieve strong performance in complex, strategic environments with combinatorial action spaces and intricate strategic interactions. The fact that DipLLM surpasses Cicero with a tiny fraction of the training data suggests a significant improvement in data efficiency. This could have implications for applying AI to other similarly complex domains where generating large datasets is impractical.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined solution (DipLLM architecture and training process).
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating superior performance compared to baselines, including the SOTA Cicero model.
    *   Theoretical analysis providing justification for the chosen learning objective and factorization framework.
    *   Ablation studies effectively isolating the contributions of different components.
    *   The paper addresses a timely and relevant research problem.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper highlights reduced data requirements, the initial reliance on DipNet for data generation through interaction and piKL-Hedge search is still a limiting factor compared to full self-play.  The degree to which this data generation impacts generalization or introduces biases is not thoroughly addressed. This is a critical point, as the quality of the data generated by DipNet is a key component of DipLLM's success.
    *   The experimental setup focuses primarily on the "no-press" variant of Diplomacy. While strategically challenging, no-press Diplomacy removes the communication aspect, which is a central element of the game. Extending the framework to incorporate communication would significantly increase the complexity and represent a more comprehensive evaluation of DipLLM's strategic capabilities.
    * The paper lacks discussion of computational resources required for fine-tuning and inference. While it highlights reduced data requirements, it doesn't discuss the cost of the LLM.
    * While comparisons are made against Cicero, a deeper analysis comparing architectural differences with DipLLM could provide more insight into specific factors attributing to DipLLM's increased performance.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on applying LLMs to strategic decision-making, particularly in multiplayer games and other complex environments. The autoregressive factorization framework and fine-tuning approach could serve as a blueprint for adapting LLMs to other challenging domains. It could also spur further research into techniques for efficiently training LLMs on limited data.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by demonstrating the feasibility of using fine-tuned LLMs for strategic decision-making in Diplomacy, achieving state-of-the-art results with significantly reduced data requirements. The autoregressive factorization framework and theoretical analysis add to the paper's strength. However, the limitations related to data generation, the focus on no-press Diplomacy, the lack of computational analysis, and comparative architectural analyses temper the overall impact and justify a score below a higher rating. Further exploring and addressing these weaknesses in future work could significantly enhance the paper's influence and justify a higher assessment.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Application-Driven Value Alignment in Agentic AI Systems: Survey and Perspectives
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09656v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Zeng, Hengshu Zhu, Chuan Qin, Han Wu, Yihang Cheng, Sirui Zhang, Xiaowei Jin, Yinuo Shen, Zhenxing Wang, Feimin Zhong, Hui Xiong
- **Abstract**: The ongoing evolution of AI paradigms has propelled AI research into the Agentic AI stage. Consequently, the focus of research has shifted from single agents and simple applications towards multi-agent autonomous decision-making and task collaboration in complex environments. As Large Language Models (LLMs) advance, their applications become more diverse and complex, leading to increasingly situational and systemic risks. This has brought significant attention to value alignment for AI agents, which aims to ensure that an agent's goals, preferences, and behaviors align with human values and societal norms. This paper reviews value alignment in agent systems within specific application scenarios. It integrates the advancements in AI driven by large models with the demands of social governance. Our review covers value principles, agent system application scenarios, and agent value alignment evaluation. Specifically, value principles are organized hierarchically from a top-down perspective, encompassing macro, meso, and micro levels. Agent system application scenarios are categorized and reviewed from a general-to-specific viewpoint. Agent value alignment evaluation systematically examines datasets for value alignment assessment and relevant value alignment methods. Additionally, we delve into value coordination among multiple agents within agent systems. Finally, we propose several potential research directions in this field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper provides a survey and perspective on application-driven value alignment in agentic AI systems. It argues that as AI evolves from single agents to multi-agent systems in complex environments, ensuring that AI agents' goals, preferences, and behaviors align with human values becomes increasingly important. The paper reviews value principles organized hierarchically (macro, meso, micro levels), categorizes and reviews agent system application scenarios from general to specific viewpoints, and examines datasets and methods for agent value alignment evaluation. It also explores value coordination among multiple agents and proposes potential research directions. The core argument is that value alignment needs to be viewed as a systemic governance issue embedded in the design of interaction mechanisms and organizational structures of agentic AI.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive review of the emerging field of value alignment *specifically within the context of agentic AI systems*. Existing surveys (cited in the paper itself) tend to focus on LLM value alignment more broadly or on specific technical aspects. This paper attempts to bridge the gap between AI safety research and the practical deployment of LLM-based agents within various application scenarios and organizational contexts. This focused approach, while a refinement of previous work, offers a more targeted and relevant assessment as agentic AI becomes more prevalent.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its timely consideration of ethical and societal implications of multi-agent AI systems. The hierarchical structuring of value principles (macro, meso, micro) is useful for systematically analyzing and addressing value alignment challenges at different levels. The organization of application scenarios by generality is also a practical framework for understanding the breadth and depth of LLM-agent deployment. The call to view value alignment as a systemic governance issue rather than merely a technical one is crucial. Highlighting the importance of value coordination in multi-agent environments is a critical contribution. The future directions outlined could help drive research towards more robust and ethically sound agentic AI systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive and well-organized survey of relevant literature.
    *   Clear and accessible explanation of core concepts.
    *   Hierarchical structuring of value principles is insightful.
    *   Highlights the critical need to move beyond single-agent perspectives.
    *   Timely discussion on value coordination in multi-agent systems.
    *   Practical focus, connecting theoretical concepts with real-world application scenarios.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper, while comprehensive, is primarily a survey. It doesn't present novel technical methods or empirical results.
    *   The discussion on organizational models could be further expanded to offer more concrete guidance on how specific organizational structures might influence value alignment implementation.
    *   The discussion on the sharing of value propositions from datasets could have benefited from a discussion on how to verify and validate this information before it is applied to downstream tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the direction of research in several ways:
    *   By providing a structured framework for analyzing value alignment challenges in agentic AI.
    *   By encouraging researchers to consider the systemic and organizational dimensions of value alignment.
    *   By motivating further research on value coordination mechanisms in multi-agent systems.
    *   By highlighting the need for more diverse and granular datasets for evaluating value alignment.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution by providing a timely and comprehensive survey of value alignment issues in the emerging field of agentic AI. It synthesizes existing research, offers a useful framework for analysis, and proposes promising future research directions. While it doesn't present entirely novel technical innovations, its focused approach on multi-agent systems within various application scenarios distinguishes it from more general value alignment surveys. Its impact lies in its ability to guide future research and policy-making in this crucial area.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Intent Factored Generation: Unleashing the Diversity in Your Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09659v1)
- **Authors**: Eltayeb Ahmed, Uljad Berdica, Martha Elliott, Danijela Horak, Jakob N. Foerster
- **Abstract**: Obtaining multiple meaningfully diverse, high quality samples from Large Language Models for a fixed prompt remains an open challenge. Current methods for increasing diversity often only operate at the token-level, paraphrasing the same response. This is problematic because it leads to poor exploration on reasoning problems and to unengaging, repetitive conversational agents. To address this we propose Intent Factored Generation (IFG), factorising the sampling process into two stages. First, we sample a semantically dense intent, e.g., a summary or keywords. Second, we sample the final response conditioning on both the original prompt and the intent from the first stage. This allows us to use a higher temperature during the intent step to promote conceptual diversity, and a lower temperature during the final generation to ensure the outputs are coherent and self-consistent. Additionally, we find that prompting the model to explicitly state its intent for each step of the chain-of-thought before generating the step is beneficial for reasoning tasks. We demonstrate our method's effectiveness across a diverse set of tasks. We show this method improves both pass@k and Reinforcement Learning from Verifier Feedback on maths and code tasks. For instruction-tuning, we combine IFG with Direct Preference Optimisation to increase conversational diversity without sacrificing reward. Finally, we achieve higher diversity while maintaining the quality of generations on a general language modelling task, using a new dataset of reader comments and news articles that we collect and open-source. In summary, we present a simple method of increasing the sample diversity of LLMs while maintaining performance. This method can be implemented by changing the prompt and varying the temperature during generation, making it easy to integrate into many algorithms for gains across various applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Intent Factored Generation: Unleashing the Diversity in Your Language Model":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of limited diversity in the responses of Large Language Models (LLMs). Current token-level diversity enhancement methods often result in mere paraphrasing rather than true semantic diversity. To overcome this, the authors propose Intent Factored Generation (IFG). IFG separates the generation process into two steps: 1) sampling a semantically dense "intent" (e.g., keywords, summary) that anchors the response, and 2) generating the final response conditioned on both the original prompt and the sampled intent. This allows for a higher temperature to be used during the intent sampling to encourage conceptual diversity, followed by a lower temperature in the final generation stage to ensure coherence.  The authors also introduce IFG-prompting, where the model explicitly states its intended purpose before generating each step in a chain-of-thought reasoning process. The method is evaluated across tasks such as mathematics problem solving, code generation, instruction tuning, and general language modeling, showing improvements in various metrics including pass@k, RLVF, and a novel metric called Relaxed Semantic Entropy (RSE). The authors also provide a new open-source dataset of Reddit news comments and present an implementation of IFG as a simple wrapper for LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of separating intent sampling from response generation is conceptually straightforward, but the paper demonstrates its practical effectiveness.  While there have been prior works on structured generation using control tokens or planning stages, IFG distinguishes itself by 1) explicitly sampling and conditioning on an interpretable "intent" learned through prompts or finetuning, 2) interspersing intent extraction and content generation (IFG-prompting), and 3) applying it to a range of reasoning and open-ended generation tasks. The introduction of Relaxed Semantic Entropy (RSE) is a valuable contribution for measuring semantic diversity in longer sequences.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the method's ability to improve exploration and semantic diversity for reasoning and conversational tasks. Improved pass@k and RLVF results on mathematical and coding tasks suggest that IFG can facilitate more effective problem-solving by increasing the chance of generating correct solutions. The demonstration of increased diversity in conversational agents without sacrificing reward indicates that IFG can address a crucial limitation of current instruction-tuned models. The technique is computationally efficient and can be applied to existing LLMs with minimal changes through few-shot prompting and temperature tuning.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is conceptually simple and easy to implement.
    *   Empirical results demonstrate improvements on a diverse set of tasks.
    *   The introduction of RSE provides a useful metric for semantic diversity.
    *   Provides the Reddit News Comments dataset, which enables for a more robust analysis of diverse model outputs.
    *   Low compute requirements
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The concept is quite simple, and the novelty is more in the application than the underlying idea.
    *   The need to prime the LLM to output intents through few-shot prompting could be a limitation in some scenarios.  The IFG-prompting approach might need careful prompt engineering for each new task.
    *   The authors only note a limitation where reasoning fails when a reliable verifier is absent (e.g., MMLU dataset), but don't explore settings where the verifier is weak or only partially correct.
    *   The quantitative evaluation of the comments dataset is relatively simplistic, focusing on coherence and diversity scores, with limited analysis of potential biases or downstream impact of the generated comments.
    *   While easy to implement and computationally inexpensive, there is an engineering complexity: prompts have to be crafted for all the applications considered.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's simplicity and effectiveness could lead to broader adoption of intent-aware generation techniques. The IFG strategy could be incorporated into other RLHF and RLVF variants, tree search algorithms, and evolutionary algorithms. The introduction of IFG-prompting may also inspire new prompting strategies that encourage more structured and fine-grained reasoning.
*   **Rigorous Rationale:** While the paper presents a strong empirical case for IFG, the impact is solid but may be limited. There were a few unconsidered risks such as weak verifiers and biases, and despite strong gains, there's not a huge leap in overall utility.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### VideoMat: Extracting PBR Materials from Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09665v1)
- **Authors**: Jacob Munkberg, Zian Wang, Ruofan Liang, Tianchang Shen, Jon Hasselgren
- **Abstract**: We leverage finetuned video diffusion models, intrinsic decomposition of videos, and physically-based differentiable rendering to generate high quality materials for 3D models given a text prompt or a single image. We condition a video diffusion model to respect the input geometry and lighting condition. This model produces multiple views of a given 3D model with coherent material properties. Secondly, we use a recent model to extract intrinsics (base color, roughness, metallic) from the generated video. Finally, we use the intrinsics alongside the generated video in a differentiable path tracer to robustly extract PBR materials directly compatible with common content creation tools.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "VideoMat," a method for extracting high-quality Physically Based Rendering (PBR) materials from video diffusion models. The approach leverages finetuned video diffusion models, intrinsic video decomposition, and differentiable rendering. The video diffusion model is conditioned on 3D geometry and lighting to generate multi-view images of a 3D model with consistent material properties. Then, an intrinsic decomposition model extracts albedo, roughness, and metallic maps. Finally, a differentiable path tracer uses this information to extract PBR materials that can be used in content creation tools. The paper shows improved quality and separation of lighting and materials compared to prior image-based diffusion model techniques. The method also supports image-based material generation and demonstrates several applications, including relighting and user studies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in combining several existing techniques—finetuned video diffusion models, intrinsic decomposition, and differentiable rendering—into a cohesive pipeline for material extraction. While each component has been explored individually, the specific combination and conditioning strategy for video diffusion models on geometry and lighting are reasonably novel. The conditioning of video diffusion models with G-buffer guides is an extension of existing image diffusion work, adapting it to the video domain. Leveraging video diffusion models for better view consistency in material extraction offers a significant advantage over image-based methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical challenge in 3D content creation: generating high-quality PBR materials from readily available resources like text prompts or single images. By addressing the view consistency issues that plague image-based diffusion models, the method can create more detailed and spatially varying materials. This has potential benefits for areas like game development, film production, and virtual reality where realistic material representation is essential. The comparisons to competing approaches (Paint-it, DreamMat) clearly show the method's strengths in demodulating base color and improving relighting accuracy.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Improved Material Quality:** The results demonstrate improved material detail and view consistency compared to image-based techniques. This is a key contribution as view consistency is a well-known issue with image diffusion models.
    *   **Coherent Pipeline:** The combination of video diffusion, intrinsic decomposition, and differentiable rendering creates a well-defined and efficient pipeline.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper uses various qualitative and quantitative evaluations including a user study, which provide compelling evidence for the effectiveness of the method.
    *   **Support for Image-based material generation:** The model also supports image-based material generation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Existing Models:** The method relies on pretrained and finetuned models (Cosmos) and a specific intrinsic decomposition network. This makes the approach somewhat dependent on the quality and availability of these models.
    *   **Limitations of Video Models:** Video models can introduce motion blur or video compression artifacts, which can impact the final material quality. The authors acknowledge this but don't fully address its impact.
    *   **Limited View Distribution:** The 360-degree orbit camera movement is a limiting factor; the model needs a more distributed camera coverage.
    *   **Complexity:** The approach combines a number of complex elements, increasing the difficulty to reproduce and adapt.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence material generation workflows by providing a more robust and consistent method for extracting PBR materials from text prompts or single images. The strategy of conditioning video diffusion models on geometry and lighting could be adopted by other researchers in the field.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field. While it doesn't introduce completely novel techniques, it successfully combines and adapts existing methods to solve a practical problem. The improvements in material quality and view consistency are significant. However, the dependency on existing models and certain limitations in view distribution prevent a higher rating. The combination of view-consistent multi-frame material prediction and the use of a differentiable path tracer contribute significantly towards material accuracy and lighting reproduction. The presented user study reinforces the quality of the work.

### Query-Level Uncertainty in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09669v1)
- **Authors**: Lihu Chen, Gaël Varoquaux
- **Abstract**: It is important for Large Language Models to be aware of the boundary of their knowledge, the mechanism of identifying known and unknown queries. This type of awareness can help models perform adaptive inference, such as invoking RAG, engaging in slow and deep thinking, or adopting the abstention mechanism, which is beneficial to the development of efficient and trustworthy AI. In this work, we propose a method to detect knowledge boundaries via Query-Level Uncertainty, which aims to determine if the model is able to address a given query without generating any tokens. To this end, we introduce a novel and training-free method called \emph{Internal Confidence}, which leverages self-evaluations across layers and tokens. Empirical results on both factual QA and mathematical reasoning tasks demonstrate that our internal confidence can outperform several baselines. Furthermore, we showcase that our proposed method can be used for efficient RAG and model cascading, which is able to reduce inference costs while maintaining performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Query-Level Uncertainty in Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the concept of "Query-Level Uncertainty" (QLU) for Large Language Models (LLMs), aiming to determine if a model can address a given query *before* generating any tokens. This is in contrast to existing approaches that focus on *answer-level* uncertainty. The authors propose a training-free method called "Internal Confidence" that leverages self-evaluations across layers and tokens to estimate QLU.  They demonstrate the effectiveness of Internal Confidence on factual QA and mathematical reasoning tasks, showing it outperforms several baselines. The paper also explores applications of QLU for efficient RAG and model cascading to reduce inference costs while maintaining performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in formally defining and operationalizing the concept of query-level uncertainty, as opposed to the more prevalent answer-level uncertainty. This shift in perspective is valuable. The "Internal Confidence" method itself, while leveraging existing concepts of self-evaluation and layer aggregation, is a relatively straightforward and innovative implementation. The application of attenuated encoding for weighting is borrowed from prior work, but adapted to this specific context.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the potential benefits of QLU in building more efficient and trustworthy AI systems. Enabling LLMs to assess their ability to answer a question *before* generating an answer is crucial for adaptive inference (e.g., triggering RAG or model cascading only when needed) and abstention (avoiding incorrect or nonsensical responses). Efficient RAG and model cascading applications provide strong evidence of the value derived from query level uncertainty scores. However, the benefits of this work are very practically oriented, so it will likely only be as impactful as the implementations that end up using it.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of QLU and its potential benefits.
    *   **Training-Free Approach:**  The proposed method is training-free, making it simple to implement and apply to a wide range of LLMs without incurring additional training costs.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results on multiple datasets and tasks provide solid evidence for the effectiveness of Internal Confidence. The application to RAG and model cascading showcases the practical utility of the method.
    *   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the concepts and methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Method Simplicity:** While the training-free aspect is a strength, the method itself is relatively simple. It raises the question if better performance is available for approaches that are training-based.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The paper's focus is on questions with definite answers. It doesn't address the challenges of QLU for open-ended or subjective queries. The paper assumes a well-curated training dataset with minimal ambiguity, so the results are not fully generalizable.
    *   **Decision Center Simplification:** Fixing the decision center to the last layer and token might not be optimal for all models and tasks. The paper justifies this simplification by avoiding the need for training data, but further exploration of adaptive decision centers could improve performance.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The selection of AUC and ECE is justified by prior work, but it would be interesting to see the method evaluated on a broader range of metrics relevant to the specific applications (e.g., cost savings in RAG and model cascading).

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research on LLM uncertainty estimation, adaptive inference, and trustworthy AI. It introduces a valuable concept (QLU) and provides a practical, training-free method for estimating it. Future work could build upon this by exploring more sophisticated methods for QLU estimation, addressing QLU for open-ended queries, and developing more advanced adaptive inference strategies based on QLU.
*   **Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by introducing and formalizing the concept of query-level uncertainty. The proposed Internal Confidence method is practical and shows promising results. The applications to RAG and model cascading add to the paper's significance. However, the simplicity of the method and the limited generalizability somewhat temper the overall impact. While the training free aspect is a plus, the results demonstrate limited novelty, hence the mid-tier score. Furthermore, there is no discussion about practical implementation aspects, such as computational cost to generate the confidence scores.

### DHoTT: A Temporal Extension of Homotopy Type Theory for Semantic Drift
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09671v1)
- **Authors**: Iman Poernomo
- **Abstract**: We introduce Dynamic Homotopy Type Theory (DHoTT), a temporal extension of Homotopy Type Theory (HoTT) designed to reason formally about concepts whose meanings evolve continuously or rupture discontinuously over time. While traditional HoTT captures identity and equivalence within a fixed semantic landscape, DHoTT enriches this framework by explicitly indexing types with a temporal parameter, allowing types themselves to deform, rupture, and reassemble as contexts shift. Formally, we show that DHoTT serves as the internal language of a presheaf topos over the linearly ordered time category. As a result, DHoTT (1) conservatively extends HoTT, recovering standard homotopy-theoretic reasoning when time is held constant; (2) preserves foundational structures such as univalence and higher inductive types; and (3) introduces new constructs (drift paths and rupture types) for precisely capturing semantic evolution and discontinuity. We illustrate the expressiveness of DHoTT through a worked example derived from conversational dynamics in large language models, highlighting its relevance to posthuman intelligence and the formal modeling of evolving meaning.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper "DHOTT: A Temporal Extension of Homotopy Type Theory for Semantic Drift." Here's a summary, critical evaluation, and score:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Dynamic Homotopy Type Theory (DHoTT), an extension of Homotopy Type Theory (HoTT) designed to formalize reasoning about the evolution of meaning and semantic drift in dynamic contexts, such as conversations mediated by large language models (LLMs).  DHoTT extends HoTT by indexing types with a context-time parameter, allowing types to deform, rupture, and reassemble. The authors demonstrate that DHoTT is the internal language of the presheaf category [(R, ≤)°P, SSet], which serves as the categorical model. Key features include conservative extension of HoTT, preservation of standard HoTT structures, and the introduction of new syntactic constructs (drift paths, rupture types, and healing cells) for reasoning about semantic evolution and discontinuities. The paper illustrates the utility of DHoTT with examples derived from conversational dynamics in LLMs, emphasizing its expressiveness and applicability to posthuman intelligence and evolving meaning research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel extension of HoTT that explicitly tackles the problem of semantic drift. While temporal logics and guarded type theories exist, they don't usually address the evolution of *types* themselves over time. The introduction of drift paths, rupture types, and healing cells is syntactically and semantically novel.  The application of presheaf categories to model this dynamic semantic evolution is a solid theoretical foundation. However, similar ideas have been used in other areas of logic and computer science; the novelty lies in the specific application and combination with HoTT.

*   **Significance:** The paper's potential impact is significant. As LLMs become more prevalent, understanding and controlling semantic drift becomes crucial for ensuring coherence and reliability. DHoTT provides a formal framework for analyzing and potentially mitigating semantic hallucinations and promoting controlled semantic shifts. The connection to practical AI workflows like prompt engineering and RAG is promising. The authors convincingly argue that classical approaches are insufficient for capturing the nuances of meaning evolution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Sound Theoretical Foundation:** The use of presheaf categories provides a strong, well-defined semantics for DHoTT. The paper convincingly demonstrates that DHoTT conservatively extends HoTT and preserves its core properties.
    *   **Clear Motivation and Examples:** The examples, particularly those related to LLMs, effectively illustrate the practical relevance of DHoTT and highlight the limitations of existing approaches. The theorem-proving example adds further weight to the usefulness of this approach.
    *   **Rigorous Development:** The paper provides a detailed and rigorous development of the syntax, semantics, and key properties of DHoTT.
    *   **Addresses an Important Problem:** Semantic drift is a significant challenge in AI, and this paper offers a promising formal solution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Practical Implementation:** The paper currently lacks concrete implementation details or a working prototype. While the theoretical framework is solid, the practical challenges of implementing DHoTT and integrating it into existing LLM workflows remain to be addressed. The performance and scalability are open questions.
    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The examples are illustrative but not empirical. The paper would benefit from experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of DHoTT in real-world scenarios. Even a limited proof-of-concept implementation with controlled LLM experiments would strengthen the paper significantly.
    *   **Complexity:** HoTT itself is a complex and niche area of logic. This added complexity of the DHOTT approach will likely limit uptake.

* **Overall Score:**
While the paper proposes a novel approach based on a theoretically sound and rigorous foundation that tackles a very important problem, it lacks implementation and empirical validation. It also builds upon an already complex framework. However, the work opens up some important avenues for future research that connects logical foundations and practically relevant AI.

### Is Fine-Tuning an Effective Solution? Reassessing Knowledge Editing for Unstructured Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09672v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Xiong, Chuanyuan Tan, Wenliang Chen
- **Abstract**: Unstructured Knowledge Editing (UKE) is crucial for updating the relevant knowledge of large language models (LLMs). It focuses on unstructured inputs, such as long or free-form texts, which are common forms of real-world knowledge. Although previous studies have proposed effective methods and tested them, some issues exist: (1) Lack of Locality evaluation for UKE, and (2) Abnormal failure of fine-tuning (FT) based methods for UKE. To address these issues, we first construct two datasets, UnKEBench-Loc and AKEW-Loc (CF), by extending two existing UKE datasets with locality test data from the unstructured and structured views. This enables a systematic evaluation of the Locality of post-edited models. Furthermore, we identify four factors that may affect the performance of FT-based methods. Based on these factors, we conduct experiments to determine how the well-performing FT-based methods should be trained for the UKE task, providing a training recipe for future research. Our experimental results indicate that the FT-based method with the optimal setting (FT-UKE) is surprisingly strong, outperforming the existing state-of-the-art (SOTA). In batch editing scenarios, FT-UKE shows strong performance as well, with its advantage over SOTA methods increasing as the batch size grows, expanding the average metric lead from +6.78% to +10.80%
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the task of Unstructured Knowledge Editing (UKE), where the goal is to update factual knowledge embedded in large language models (LLMs) given unstructured text. The authors identify two key issues with existing UKE research: a lack of dedicated datasets for evaluating the "Locality" of edits (i.e., whether unrelated knowledge is preserved) and the surprisingly poor performance of fine-tuning (FT) based methods compared to their success in structured knowledge editing. To address these, the authors:

1.  Construct two new datasets, UnKEBench-Loc and AKEW-Loc (CF), by extending existing UKE datasets with locality test data covering unstructured and structured views.

2.  Analyze the factors influencing the performance of FT-based methods in UKE, including loss calculation scope, layer selection, component selection, and chat template.

3.  Conduct experiments to determine the optimal settings for FT-based methods, developing a training recipe.

4.  Demonstrate that the fine-tuned method with optimal settings (FT-UKE) significantly outperforms state-of-the-art (SOTA) UKE methods and maintains its advantage even in batch editing scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The paper's novelty lies primarily in:

1.  **Dataset Contribution:**  The creation of  UnKEBench-Loc and AKEW-Loc (CF) directly addresses a key gap in UKE evaluation. While existing datasets primarily focus on edit success and generalization, the new datasets explicitly incorporate locality test data, which is crucial for practical applications where preserving existing knowledge is vital. The inclusion of multi-type locality data (unstructured and structured) further strengthens the dataset.
2.  **Systematic Analysis of FT Methods:** The paper's systematic breakdown of FT-based methods into distinct factors (loss calculation scope, layer selection, etc.) is insightful. By analyzing these factors through controlled experiments, the authors provide a valuable training recipe for FT in UKE, which was lacking in previous research. This component provides a clearer understanding of FT in this specific domain.

*Significance:*

The significance of the paper stems from:

1.  **Improved Evaluation:** The new datasets will likely become benchmarks for future UKE research, allowing for more rigorous and comprehensive evaluation of methods.

2.  **Advancement of FT Methods:** The demonstration that a properly configured FT-based method (FT-UKE) can significantly outperform existing SOTA methods is a major contribution. It challenges the prevailing view that FT is not well-suited for UKE and provides a promising avenue for future research by establishing a stronger baseline.
3. **Batch Editing Performance:** Showing the consistent advantages of FT-UKE even in batch editing scenarios helps to demonstrate a stronger robustness and broader applicability of their methods.

*Strengths:*

*   **Well-defined Problem and Clear Motivation:**  The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing UKE research and provides a strong rationale for the proposed contributions.

*   **Rigorous Methodology:** The experimental design is well-structured, with controlled experiments to analyze the impact of different factors on FT performance.

*   **Significant Performance Gains:** The FT-UKE method achieves substantial performance improvements over existing SOTA methods, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the proposed methods and experimental results.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Scope of FT Configuration Exploration:** While the paper explores several important factors influencing FT performance, the search space of possible configurations is still vast.  The paper admits limitations in computing resources which means further improvements might be found with more exhaustive searches.

*   **Generalizability of Findings:** While the experiments are conducted on two different LLMs, it is important to acknowledge that the optimal FT configurations may vary depending on the specific model architecture and training data.

*   **Relatively incremental contribution.** While there is a clear performance boost, it does rely on a foundation of established techniques, leading it to be less novel than a truly groundbreaking method that required a completely new approach.

*Score:*

Score: 7.5

*Rationale:*

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of UKE by addressing two key limitations: the lack of dedicated datasets for locality evaluation and the suboptimal performance of FT-based methods. The new datasets and the systematic analysis of FT factors will likely have a lasting impact on future research. While the contribution is incremental in some ways (building upon established techniques), the significant performance gains achieved by FT-UKE and the valuable training recipe provided justify a high score. The limitations regarding generalizability and scope of configuration exploration prevent a higher score.  The paper represents a solid and impactful advancement in the field, but the relatively small jump in conceptual novelty restricts it to this score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reasoning Models Are More Easily Gaslighted Than You Think
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09677v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Zhu, Hailong Yin, Jingjing Chen, Yu-Gang Jiang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in reasoning-centric models promise improved robustness through mechanisms such as chain-of-thought prompting and test-time scaling. However, their ability to withstand misleading user input remains underexplored. In this paper, we conduct a systematic evaluation of three state-of-the-art reasoning models, i.e., OpenAI's o4-mini, Claude-3.7-Sonnet and Gemini-2.5-Flash, across three multimodal benchmarks: MMMU, MathVista, and CharXiv. Our evaluation reveals significant accuracy drops (25-29% on average) following gaslighting negation prompts, indicating that even top-tier reasoning models struggle to preserve correct answers under manipulative user feedback. Built upon the insights of the evaluation and to further probe this vulnerability, we introduce GaslightingBench-R, a new diagnostic benchmark specifically designed to evaluate reasoning models' susceptibility to defend their belief under gaslighting negation prompt. Constructed by filtering and curating 1,025 challenging samples from the existing benchmarks, GaslightingBench-R induces even more dramatic failures, with accuracy drops exceeding 53% on average. Our findings reveal fundamental limitations in the robustness of reasoning models, highlighting the gap between step-by-step reasoning and belief persistence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the vulnerability of state-of-the-art reasoning models (OpenAI's 04-mini, Claude-3.7-Sonnet, and Gemini-2.5-Flash) to "gaslighting," defined as the manipulation of a model's beliefs through misleading negation prompts. The authors evaluate these models on three multimodal benchmarks (MMMU, MathVista, CharXiv), showing significant accuracy drops when models are challenged with false user feedback after initially providing a correct answer. The paper also introduces GaslightingBench-R, a new benchmark specifically designed to expose this vulnerability, demonstrating even more dramatic failures in belief persistence. The findings reveal a gap between reasoning transparency (achieved through chain-of-thought) and actual reasoning robustness, suggesting that current models struggle to maintain correct answers under adversarial manipulation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper tackles an interesting and relatively underexplored aspect of reasoning model robustness: the ability to maintain correct beliefs under adversarial questioning. While prior work has examined sycophancy and susceptibility to misleading prompts, this paper specifically focuses on *gaslighting* through negation and its impact on reasoning models, which adds a new dimension. The creation of GaslightingBench-R is also a novel contribution, providing a targeted benchmark for assessing this specific vulnerability.

*   **Significance:** The findings have potentially significant implications for the deployment of reasoning models in real-world scenarios. If models can be easily manipulated into reversing correct answers, their reliability and trustworthiness are questionable, particularly in domains where human input may be unreliable or intentionally misleading. The paper highlights the need for developing more robust reasoning mechanisms that are resistant to adversarial manipulation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly presents the problem, methodology, and results.
    *   The use of multiple state-of-the-art reasoning models and diverse multimodal benchmarks adds to the credibility of the findings.
    *   The introduction of GaslightingBench-R is a valuable contribution, providing a targeted tool for future research in this area.
    *   The qualitative examples effectively illustrate the models' failure modes and the generation of hallucinated rationales.
    *   The paper highlights an important limitation of current "reasoning" models: the reasoning is more about generating plausible text given some context, and not about true, belief-anchored reasoning.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The gaslighting prompt used is fairly simple ("No, that's incorrect. Please verify your answer."). It's possible that more sophisticated gaslighting techniques (e.g., providing plausible but incorrect alternative reasoning) could yield different results.
    *   The paper primarily focuses on *demonstrating* the vulnerability, rather than *addressing* it. While the findings are important, the paper doesn't offer concrete solutions or strategies for improving model robustness to gaslighting.
    *   It would be valuable to investigate *why* the models are susceptible. Is it a lack of grounding in external knowledge? A bias toward agreeing with user input? Understanding the underlying causes could inform the development of more effective defense mechanisms.
    *   The benchmark is curated with a model-driven approach. While useful for the current purpose, it could introduce some biases in the benchmark itself.
    *   The paper does not compare with smaller models, which might have lower performance to begin with, but might demonstrate better reasoning for what they know.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to stimulate further research on adversarial robustness in reasoning models. GaslightingBench-R could become a standard benchmark for evaluating the susceptibility of new models to this type of manipulation. The findings may also influence the design of new training methods or architectures that promote belief persistence and resistance to adversarial feedback.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper tackles an important and novel issue, it primarily focuses on demonstrating the vulnerability rather than proposing solutions. The simplicity of the gaslighting prompt and the model-driven nature of the benchmark are minor limitations. However, the paper is well-executed, presents compelling evidence, and is likely to spur further research in this area. The insights regarding the divergence between reasoning transparency and robustness are particularly valuable.

### Assessing the Quality of Denoising Diffusion Models in Wasserstein Distance: Noisy Score and Optimal Bounds
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09681v1)
- **Authors**: Vahan Arsenyan, Elen Vardanyan, Arnak Dalalyan
- **Abstract**: Generative modeling aims to produce new random examples from an unknown target distribution, given access to a finite collection of examples. Among the leading approaches, denoising diffusion probabilistic models (DDPMs) construct such examples by mapping a Brownian motion via a diffusion process driven by an estimated score function. In this work, we first provide empirical evidence that DDPMs are robust to constant-variance noise in the score evaluations. We then establish finite-sample guarantees in Wasserstein-2 distance that exhibit two key features: (i) they characterize and quantify the robustness of DDPMs to noisy score estimates, and (ii) they achieve faster convergence rates than previously known results. Furthermore, we observe that the obtained rates match those known in the Gaussian case, implying their optimality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the performance and robustness of Denoising Diffusion Probabilistic Models (DDPMs) when the score function used for generation is corrupted by noise.  The authors show empirically that DDPMs are surprisingly resilient to noisy score evaluations. They provide theoretical finite-sample guarantees in the Wasserstein-2 distance, highlighting the robustness to noisy score estimates and achieving faster convergence rates than previous results. The authors also show the convergence rates match known rates in the Gaussian case, which is considered optimal. The analysis extends beyond Gaussian distributions to compactly supported semi-log-concave measures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty stems from several aspects: 1) the empirical demonstration and theoretical justification of DDPM robustness to noisy score evaluations, an aspect that hasn't been extensively studied. 2) Improved convergence rates in Wasserstein-2 distance compared to prior work, specifically achieving an optimal rate that matches the Gaussian case. 3) Extends the analysis to a broader class of distributions than previously considered in works with similar convergence rates.

*   **Significance:** The paper contributes significantly to the theoretical understanding of DDPMs. Demonstrating robustness to noisy scores is valuable because it reflects real-world scenarios where score estimates might be imperfect (e.g., due to compression or communication errors). The improved convergence rates and broader applicability of the analysis are important for practitioners who want guarantees on the quality of generated samples. The result on optimally closing the gap by proving the lower bound is crucial in establishing that the DDPM procedure is sound. The ability to extend DDPM performance analysis to scenarios with log-concave distributions with bounded support greatly enhances applicability in real-world scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical evidence supporting the theoretical findings.
    *   Rigorous mathematical analysis with clear proofs.
    *   Well-defined problem statement and conditions.
    *   Comparison with previous work that clearly highlights the improvements made.
    *   The paper tackles an important problem in generative modeling with broad practical implications.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The assumptions on the target distribution, while relaxed compared to *some* prior work, might still be restrictive for certain real-world applications. The boundedness assumption, while general, still may not be the case in all real-world scenarios.
    *   The analysis is primarily focused on theoretical convergence rates. It may benefit from exploring the practical implications of the constant factors in the bounds, which often dominate in high-dimensional settings.

*   **Justification of score:** The paper presents a valuable and novel contribution. The results establish robustness properties and improved convergence rates for a crucial class of generative models. While the assumptions have limited real-world applicability, the overall theoretical contribution is sound and a step in the right direction. The convergence and upper bound results match the optimality for DDPM processes, further solidifying the paper's standing.

### Inv-Entropy: A Fully Probabilistic Framework for Uncertainty Quantification in Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09684v1)
- **Authors**: Haoyi Song, Ruihan Ji, Naichen Shi, Fan Lai, Raed Al Kontar
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have transformed natural language processing, but their reliable deployment requires effective uncertainty quantification (UQ). Existing UQ methods are often heuristic and lack a probabilistic foundation. This paper begins by providing a theoretical justification for the role of perturbations in UQ for LLMs. We then introduce a dual random walk perspective, modeling input-output pairs as two Markov chains with transition probabilities defined by semantic similarity. Building on this, we propose a fully probabilistic framework based on an inverse model, which quantifies uncertainty by evaluating the diversity of the input space conditioned on a given output through systematic perturbations. Within this framework, we define a new uncertainty measure, Inv-Entropy. A key strength of our framework is its flexibility: it supports various definitions of uncertainty measures, embeddings, perturbation strategies, and similarity metrics. We also propose GAAP, a perturbation algorithm based on genetic algorithms, which enhances the diversity of sampled inputs. In addition, we introduce a new evaluation metric, Temperature Sensitivity of Uncertainty (TSU), which directly assesses uncertainty without relying on correctness as a proxy. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Inv-Entropy outperforms existing semantic UQ methods. The code to reproduce the results can be found at https://github.com/UMDataScienceLab/Uncertainty-Quantification-for-LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Inv-Entropy," a novel, fully probabilistic framework for uncertainty quantification (UQ) in large language models (LLMs). The framework is based on a dual random walk model, where input-output pairs are treated as Markov chains governed by semantic similarity.  It proposes quantifying uncertainty by assessing the diversity of inputs that could lead to a specific output, using systematic perturbations. A new uncertainty measure, Inv-Entropy, is defined within this framework.  The paper also presents GAAP, a genetic algorithm-based perturbation algorithm to improve input diversity, and TSU (Temperature Sensitivity of Uncertainty), a new metric for directly evaluating UQ without relying on correctness as a proxy. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of Inv-Entropy compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel probabilistic framework for UQ, which contrasts with existing heuristic methods. The random walk approach and the idea of quantifying uncertainty by considering input diversity given an output are both interesting and contribute to the field. The GAAP algorithm also adds a practical component for better sampling the input space. The TSU metric addresses a significant challenge in UQ evaluation and is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** LLMs are being deployed in increasingly critical applications. The paper's focus on robust UQ is therefore highly relevant. The proposed framework is flexible and can be adapted to different models, tasks, and evaluation metrics. Empirical results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance. The development of GAAP for improved perturbations and TSU for correctness-agnostic evaluation enhance the impact and applicability of the framework.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong theoretical grounding in random walk theory, providing a solid foundation for perturbation-based UQ.
    *   Flexibility of the framework allows for the use of various UQ measures, embeddings, perturbation strategies, and similarity metrics.
    *   Introduction of GAAP, which enhances the diversity of sampled inputs and significantly improves perturbation-based UQ.
    *   Novel TSU metric enables evaluation of UQ on any dataset, even when labels are unavailable.
    *   Extensive experimental results demonstrating that Inv-Entropy outperforms existing semantic UQ methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The computational cost of perturbation and replication based methods can be high, limiting scalability. The paper acknowledges this limitation and suggests adaptive perturbation strategies, but this is an area for future research.
    *   While the paper demonstrates good performance across several datasets, more analysis of the specific types of errors that Inv-Entropy is better at identifying would strengthen the paper.
    *   While TSU addresses a key limitation of correctness-based metrics, it's a relative measure. A more intuitive absolute scale or interpretation for TSU scores would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field of UQ for LLMs. The probabilistic framework offers a new perspective and provides a solid theoretical foundation for future research. GAAP and TSU provide valuable tools for improving and evaluating UQ methods.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper addresses a critical problem in LLM deployment with a well-grounded and novel approach. The combination of a probabilistic framework, a tailored perturbation algorithm (GAAP), and a new evaluation metric (TSU) makes a significant contribution. While the computational cost of perturbation methods is a limitation, the paper acknowledges this and proposes directions for future work. The clarity of presentation and the thoroughness of the experimental evaluation also contribute to the paper's value.
